  - **Replaced by `test/big64test.asm` (new, 2026-07-26), a self-contained, byte-level >64K file I/O regression test — build-verified, awaiting its first hardware round.** Built at the user's own suggestion after the diagnostic's failure, on the reasoning that trying to catch the bug mid-flight from inside the kernel had already failed once and added real risk (kernel margin, a second embarrassing diagnostic bug); a userland program that writes/reads/appends/seeks a real >64K file and verifies every byte, followed by a real `fsck.fat` pass, is strictly stronger evidence and touches no kernel code at all.
    - **Methodology**: a single running 32-bit position counter (`bt_pos`) drives a fully deterministic pattern — `pattern(pos) = the 4 bytes of pos, XORed together` — so the file's entire expected content at any position is cheaply recomputable without ever holding the whole file in RAM; only one 500-byte scratch buffer is needed. Every `K_FILE_READ`/`K_FILE_WRITE` moves exactly one chunk, and `bt_pos` is advanced by exactly that amount each time, so it always names the absolute position of the next byte.
    - **Phases, in fixed order**: (A) create + write 60000 bytes (a clean baseline safely under 65536); (B) re-open read-only and verify all 60000 bytes — confirms the baseline before ever touching the boundary; (C) re-open in append mode and write 12000 more bytes, continuing the *same* position sequence from 60000 — crosses 65536 partway through phase C's own 24 chunks, the exact shape of the original bug report (repeated `ATEST` appends crossing 65536 mid-run); (D) re-open read-only and verify **all** 72000 bytes from position 0 — catches both "is the appended tail correct" and "did the original 60000 bytes survive an append that crossed the boundary" in one pass; (E) 11 `K_FILE_SEEK` checks (`SET`/`CUR`/`END`, each with at least one case landing within the first 64K and one beyond it, plus 3 error cases — past-EOF, negative-via-`SEEK_END`, negative-via-`SEEK_CUR`) — verified by seeking then reading a few bytes and checking them against the pattern, **not** by trusting `K_FILE_SEEK`'s own returned position, which is documented to return only the low word and is therefore ambiguous for any of this test's real positions ≥ 65536; (F) final PASS/FAIL summary, with an explicit reminder printed to run `fsck` immediately afterward regardless of outcome.
    - **A full-file verify pass (B/D) that hits a short/zero read is treated as distinct and immediately fatal for that phase** — printed with the exact `bt_pos` where it happened (the single most useful diagnostic value if the cluster-chain-truncation bug reproduces, since it pins down exactly how far into the file real data stops) rather than silently continuing with position tracking that would no longer match the real file. An ordinary **data** mismatch (read succeeded, byte value is wrong) is deliberately non-fatal — verification continues through the whole pass, tallying a 32-bit total mismatch count plus the position/expected/actual of only the *first* mismatch, so a badly corrupted region doesn't flood the console but a total scope is still visible.
    - **Every seek-check position/offset was independently computed and cross-checked in Python** (32-bit two's-complement arithmetic for the negative `SEEK_CUR`/`SEEK_END` offsets) before being hand-transcribed into `ldi`/`phi`/`plo` sequences, then **re-verified by parsing the actual assembled source back out and re-simulating every `ldi`→`phi`/`plo` load sequence for `RA`/`R9`** against the same expected table — this second, independent pass caught a real transcription bug the first Python pass couldn't have (it only validated the *target* numbers, not what the hand-written assembly actually loaded): two of the eighteen load sequences (`S4`, `S5`'s own "target = 67000" loads) used a leftover `ldi 0 / ldi 1 / phi ra / plo ra` shorthand (valid only when `RA`'s high and low bytes are equal, e.g. `RA=$FFFF` via one `ldi $FF`) where the high and low bytes actually differ (`RA` needed to be `$0001`, not `$0101`) — both `phi`/`plo` ended up loaded from the *same* `ldi 1`, producing `RA=$0101` instead of `$0001`. Fixed by restoring the two-`ldi` form (`ldi 0/phi ra` then `ldi 1/plo ra`, matching the already-correct `S2`/`S6` sites) and re-running the parse-and-check script clean across all 18 sites.
    - **A second real bug caught during manual register-liveness review** (beyond what the D-clobber/self-overwrite sweeps check): `bt_short_b`/`bt_short_d` (the short-read fatal handlers) called `K_FILE_CLOSE` *before* `bt_print_short_read`, which itself stashed `RC` (the real transferred byte count, needed for the "(got N bytes)" diagnostic text) — but `K_FILE_CLOSE`'s own clobber footprint isn't documented/proven safe for `RC` (gotcha #8/#10's standing "don't trust an unaudited call" rule), so the printed byte count could have been garbage on a real short read. Fixed by moving the stash to `btB_loop`/`btD_loop` themselves, immediately after the `K_FILE_READ` call that could detect the shortfall and *before* the short-read branch (and therefore before any `K_FILE_CLOSE`) ever runs.

Links cleanly against `lib/fmt32.asm`'s `fmt_size32` (needed since most of this test's interesting positions are ≥ 65536, past plain `f_uintout`'s 16-bit range) via a new explicit Makefile rule (`test/bin/big64test: test/big64test.prg lib/fmt32.prg`, same "explicit rule beats the generic pattern rule" mechanism `bin/xcopy`/`bumptest`/`malloctest`/`envtest` already established) — `Highest address` `$5aae`, comfortably within ordinary program RAM, zero kernel-resident cost. Full clean `make`+`make progs`+`make test` (kernel byte-unchanged at `$42fa`, 31/9 `bin`/`test/bin` binaries present). **Windows build files (`Makefile.win`/`build_test.bat`) deliberately NOT updated for this program** — `lib\fmt32.prg` isn't wired up there at all yet (a pre-existing gap that would already affect `bin\dir`/`bin\stat` identically, unrelated to this change and untestable in this environment) — noted as a known gap rather than guessed at.

(every write + the close all reported success). **Phase D failed with a genuine I/O error** (`K_FILE_READ` itself returned `DF=1`, not a short/zero-count read) partway through the 72000-byte re-verify. `fsck.fat` immediately after: `"File size is 72000 bytes, cluster chain length is 65536 bytes. Truncating file to 65536 bytes."` — confirms exactly the same failure shape as the original bug report, now with a clean, trusted repro (no possibility of the earlier diagnostic's own bug muddying the result, since this round used none). The directory entry's size (72000) is correct; the FAT cluster chain backing it is short by exactly the amount past 65536 — the write path silently failed to persist chain growth past that point while still reporting success on every call.

The FCB_FPOS/FCB_FSIZE 32-bit growth-tracking code (this session's own >64K widening) doesn't influence the cluster-crossing DECISION at all — that logic only ever looks at `FCB_BOFF`/`FCB_CSECT`/`FCB_CCLUST`, none of which depend on the file's overall size. No smoking gun found by reading alone.
    - **Second-round diagnostic added to `kernel/file.asm`'s `file_write`, learning directly from the first diagnostic's own bug**: two observation points, both printing via raw BIOS calls (`f_inmsg`/`f_msg`/`f_uintout`), placed right at the cluster-crossing decision itself — (1) immediately after `call fat_get` (the "does the chain already continue, or do we need a fresh allocation" check), prints `FCB_FPOS` (high:low, two separate `f_uintout` calls since this is kernel code with no `lib/fmt32.asm` linkage) and `fat_get`'s own raw return value; (2) immediately after `call fat_alloc` succeeds, prints the newly allocated cluster number. **Every value is loaded into `RD` (not `R9`) immediately before its own `f_uintout` call** — the exact fix for the first diagnostic's bug — and `fat_get`'s/`fat_alloc`'s real result is stashed to a dedicated 2-byte scratch (`wcx_fatget`) *before* any of the printing (which itself reuses `RD` as scratch) and reloaded into `RD` right before falling through to the real EOC-check/cluster-link code, so no real logic changes. New `_wcx_diag_data` scratch proc (`wcx_fatget`/`wcx_buf`), new `extrn`s, matching the first diagnostic's own file layout/removal precedent.
    - **This targets a specific hypothesis worth stating explicitly**: if `fat_get` ever incorrectly returns a "valid next cluster" value (not the real end-of-chain marker) when the chain has genuinely ended, `file_write` would silently treat the chain as "already continuing" and skip `fat_alloc` entirely — writing believed-successful bytes into whatever cluster that bogus value names (silently corrupting something else, or free space) while `FCB_FPOS`/`FCB_FSIZE` keep advancing normally, exactly matching "every call reports success, but the real chain never grows and something eventually gets corrupted." The new diagnostic's first observation point (`fatget=`) directly tests this — if it ever prints a plausible-looking cluster number at the crossing where the corruption begins, instead of the real `$FFF8+` EOC range, that's the bug. If instead `fatget=` correctly shows EOC every time and `alloc=` fires and prints seemingly-reasonable new cluster numbers throughout, the bug is downstream of both (most likely in `fat_set`'s own link-write or `fat_flush`'s cache-eviction/write-back), and the next round should add a third observation point there.

    - **Second hardware round (2026-07-27): the second diagnostic printed NOTHING AT ALL — zero `WCX` lines, even during phase A/early phase C, which are known-good.** The user rebuilt clean, re-transferred, and reinstalled from scratch specifically to rule out a stale-binary explanation (a real possibility this project has hit before) before reporting this. Since `WCX` only fires *inside* the "did we cross a cluster boundary" branch, printing nothing at all means either that branch's own condition (`FCB_CSECT == bpb_spc`) never evaluates true anywhere in the whole 72000-byte test, or the surrounding sector-wrap block itself is never reached — both surprising, since phase A alone needs 100+ sector-boundary crossings and is independently known to work (phase B verified it byte-for-byte). This directly resolves the ambiguity from the second round's silence: it will show (a) whether this code path is reached at all during the test (it must be, many times, if phase A genuinely works the way phase B says it does), (b) the real, actual `bpb_spc` (cluster size) on this card, which was never independently confirmed and is central to interpreting everything else — if `bpb_spc` turns out unexpectedly large (e.g. 128, giving a 65536-byte cluster), the whole file could plausibly fit in ONE cluster except for the very last append, which would reframe the entire investigation around a single specific crossing rather than a general pattern. Same `RD`-immediately-before-`f_uintout` discipline as the second round; reuses the existing `wcx_fatget`/`wcx_buf` scratch (sequential, non-overlapping use within one call, no conflict with the second round's own diagnostics later in the same routine).
    - **Verified**: clean assemble (0 errors/warnings), D-clobber sweep 0 hits, self-overwrite sweep 29 hits (all pre-existing, none in the new code), no duplicate labels, all 42 `K_*` jump-table slots decoded and confirmed `0xC0`/`lbr`, `krnboot.bin` unchanged (1536 bytes), full clean `make`+`make progs`+`make test`, 31/9 `bin`/`test/bin` present. **Kernel margin is now genuinely tight from three stacked diagnostic rounds**: `Highest address` `$44e9`, **only 279 bytes before `PROG_BASE`/`KERN_LOAD`, 256 bytes boot headroom** — still positive/boot-safe, but this is close enough to the edge that a fourth round of additions should trim something first (or accept the diagnostics will need removing before any other kernel work resumes).
    - **Third hardware round (2026-07-27, `b64c.txt`): `WSX` fired reliably (117 lines in phase A, 23 in phase C) and reported `spc=128` on every single line, with zero variation. Phase A's `csect` climbed continuously 1→117 with no reset; phase C's `csect` restarted at 1, NOT continuing from 117.** The user immediately (and correctly) distrusted `spc=128` — a 512MB card would ordinarily be expected around `spc=16`. Investigated the BPB-read path directly rather than taking the printed value at face value: confirmed `boot/krnboot.asm`'s inlined `bpb_init` reads sectors-per-cluster from the textbook-correct boot-sector byte offset (`BPB_SPC equ $0D`, right after the 2-byte bytes-per-sector field at `$0B`) — so `128` is very plausibly the *real* on-disk value (FAT16 volumes in the 512MB range routinely need ≥32KB clusters just to keep the total cluster count under FAT16's ~65525-cluster ceiling — an 8KB/`spc=16` cluster size would actually overflow that limit for a 512MB volume, so a formatter choosing 64KB/`spc=128` clusters here is legitimate, not obviously wrong).
    - **The real anomaly is elsewhere, and it's a clean mathematical contradiction.** With `spc=128` genuinely active, `fopen_check_append`'s own repositioning arithmetic — for this test's real `FSIZE=60000` (`sector_index=117`) — computes `sector_in_clust = 117 & (128-1) = 117 & 127 = 117` (hand-verified byte-for-byte, see the previous round's trace). That should set `FCB_CSECT=117` at the start of phase C, and the very first sector-wrap during phase C's writes should print `WSX csect=118`. **It printed `csect=1` instead** — proof `FCB_CSECT` actually started phase C at 0, not 117. Since `117 & (N-1) = 0` for an odd number like 117 has no solution among the FAT16-legal power-of-two `spc` values *except* `N=1` (as `spc-1=0` masks everything to 0 regardless of the other operand), this is only reconcilable if `bpb_spc` read back as **1**, not 128, at the exact moment `fopen_check_append`'s masking runs — even though `file_write`'s own loop (reached moments later, in the same call) reliably reads the correct 128. Something is leaving a stale/wrong BPB active specifically during `file_open`'s own processing, distinct from whatever's active once `file_write`'s own `_switch_drive` call runs.
    - **Fourth-round diagnostic**: removed all three prior diagnostics entirely (they'd done their job — confirmed the crossing-check itself works and pinned down the real `spc`) and added one new, small, targeted print, `APX spc=<N> shift=<N>`, right at the very top of `fopen_check_append` — before any of its own arithmetic runs — testing exactly this hypothesis directly rather than continuing to infer it. `file_open` itself never calls `_switch_drive` (confirmed via grep — only `file_close`/`file_read`/`file_write`/`file_seek` do); it relies entirely on `path_resolve`'s own internal switch, called once near `file_open`'s own start. If `APX` reports `spc=1` here (matching the math above), the bug is somewhere in that gap — either `path_resolve` isn't actually switching to the right drive for this call shape (a bare filename, no `X:` prefix, resolved against `cur_dir`), or something between its switch and `fopen_check_append` resets/never-initializes the active BPB. If `APX` instead reports `128` (matching `file_write`'s own later read), the contradiction is even stranger and points at `fopen_check_append`'s own arithmetic misreading its input despite `bpb_spc` itself being correct — worth a closer line-by-line re-trace of the masking step specifically if that's what comes back.

This one line should be decisive. Remove the diagnostic (plus `_wcx_diag_data`/its `extrn`s) once root-caused (`grep -rn "TEMPORARY\|DIAGNOSTIC" kernel/file.asm` before calling it done). **Same standing warning**: disposable test data only, given the demonstrated severity.
  - **⭐ ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED (2026-07-27, `b64d.txt`) — this closes out the >64K bug hunt, pending one final confirming hardware round.** The fourth-round `APX` diagnostic actually printed **twice**, not once, and that was the key: `"APX spc=16 shift=4"` fired first, *before* `BIG64TEST`'s own startup banner even printed — this is the shell's own `hist_append` opening `history.dat` in append mode to log the just-typed command, on the boot/shell drive (a smaller, differently-formatted partition — `spc=16` there is entirely plausible and unrelated to this bug). Then `"APX spc=128 shift=7"` fired exactly where expected, for `BIG64TST.DAT`'s own real append open on the 512MB `F:` drive — **matching `file_write`'s own later read exactly**, refuting the third round's own "`bpb_spc` must read as 1" deduction (that deduction was sound math, but built on the wrong premise that only one `fopen_check_append` call was happening).
  - **With `spc=128` now confirmed correct at every point, the earlier "mathematically must be 1" contradiction meant the bug had to be in the arithmetic itself, not in which BPB is active — a full byte-by-byte re-trace of `fopen_check_append` (independent of any prior assumption) found it in minutes.** The `cluster_index = sector_index >> spc_shift` computation and the `sector_in_clust = sector_index & (spc-1)` computation are two separate blocks that were written in this order in the source — but `cluster_index`'s own shift loop (`fa_cidx_shr`) uses `R7` as its working accumulator, **shifting it in place** and leaving it holding `cluster_index`, not `sector_index`, by the time it falls through to `fa_cidx_done`. The `sector_in_clust` block right after it does `glo r7` expecting `sector_index` (per its own comment, "D = sector_index's low byte") — but `R7` had already been overwritten. For this test's real values (`sector_index=117`, `spc_shift=7`), `cluster_index` computes to `0` (117 fits in one 65536-byte cluster, correctly), and `sector_in_clust` then computed `0 & 127 = 0` instead of the correct `117 & 127 = 117` — **exactly matching every observed symptom**: `FCB_CSECT` starting phase C at 0 (not 117), every subsequent write landing in the wrong sector-within-cluster (overwriting sector 0 of the file's own single cluster repeatedly instead of genuinely extending past it), the real on-disk cluster chain staying frozen at exactly 65536 bytes while `FCB_FPOS`/`FCB_FSIZE`'s own separate, correct arithmetic kept advancing to 72000, and `fsck`'s exact `"cluster chain length is 65536 bytes"` report.
  - **Why this was invisible on every card tested before this session, for a purely mechanical reason, not luck of the data**: every prior test card had `spc=1` (confirmed by this project's own extensive prior hardware history). With `spc=1`, `bpb_spc_shift=0`, and the `fa_cidx_shr` loop's own condition (`glo r9 / lbz fa_cidx_done`) exits *immediately*, before the loop body — which is the only part of the routine that touches `R7` — ever runs once. `R7` therefore stays untouched, still correctly holding `sector_index`, and `sector_in_clust`'s `glo r7` reads the right value purely because the destructive step never fired. The exact same source code is correct for `spc=1` and silently wrong for every `spc>1` — this is why no `ATEST`/`WTEST`/append test on any earlier, smaller-cluster card ever surfaced it, and why a 512MB card (the first one in this project's history needing `spc>1` at all) was exactly what it took to expose it.
  - **Fix**: relocated the `sector_in_clust` computation to run immediately after `sector_index` is established (right after the `>>1` SHRC chain completes), *before* the `cluster_index` shift loop gets anywhere near `R7` — a pure reordering, zero new storage, using `R7` while it's still guaranteed to hold `sector_index`. The old, now-dead post-loop copy was deleted outright rather than left as dead code. **Applied identically to `file_seek`'s own `fsk_general` positioning code** (`kernel/file.asm`, the `K_FILE_SEEK` implementation) — confirmed via direct re-reading to be a byte-for-byte parallel copy of the exact same algorithm (this project's own history already documents it as "a close copy of `file_open`'s own... algorithm... deliberately kept as a second, separate copy" rather than a shared subroutine), and therefore carrying the identical bug, not yet exercised by any test that's reached far enough to trigger it (`big64test`'s own phase E seek checks never ran, since phase D's read failure ends the program first).
  - **Verified**: independently re-derived and Python-simulated both the buggy and fixed algorithms (not just re-read the assembly) — confirmed the buggy version reproduces `sector_in_clust=0` for this exact test's real inputs (`FSIZE=60000, spc=128`) and the fixed version correctly gives `117`; confirmed the fixed decomposition invariant (`cluster_index*spc + sector_in_clust == sector_index`, `0 <= sector_in_clust < spc`) holds across 200,000 randomized `(FSIZE, spc)` pairs spanning every FAT16-legal `spc` value; separately confirmed the buggy and fixed versions produce *identical* output for `spc=1` specifically, mechanically proving the "invisible until now" explanation rather than just asserting it. Clean assemble (0 errors/warnings), D-clobber sweep 0 hits, self-overwrite sweep 29 hits (all pre-existing, none in the fix's own line ranges in either copy), no duplicate labels. All 42 `K_*` jump-table slots decoded from the built `kernel.bin` and confirmed `0xC0`/`lbr`. `krnboot.bin` byte-identical (1536 bytes) — zero boot-code impact. Full clean `make`+`make progs`+`make test`, 31/9 `bin`/`test/bin` present. All temporary diagnostics (this round's `APX` and every earlier round's) fully removed — `grep -rn "TEMPORARY\|DIAGNOSTIC" kernel/file.asm` clean. **The fix is essentially free**: `Highest address` `$42f9`, **775 bytes before `PROG_BASE`/`KERN_LOAD`** — 1 byte better than the very first pre-diagnostic baseline (`$42fa`/774 bytes) from earlier in this same session, confirming the fix genuinely costs nothing (a pure instruction reordering, no new code).
  - **✅ CONFIRMED WORKING ON HARDWARE (2026-07-27) — closes out the entire multi-day, multi-round >64K file I/O bug hunt.** The user tested across **four differently-sized partitions — 32MB, 512MB, 1GB, and 2GB** (deliberately covering a wide spread of real-world `bpb_spc` values, not just the one 512MB card that originally exposed the bug) — `big64test` passed cleanly on every one (phases A through E, including the seek checks that had never once been reached before this fix), and `fsck` came back clean every time, no truncation, no corruption. This is the strongest evidence standard this project has applied to any single bug this session: a purpose-built byte-level test plus an independent filesystem-consistency check, repeated across a deliberately varied set of real cluster sizes. The working tree is now in the state the user's original "get to the bottom of it, then push and branch" plan was waiting for — see the roadmap memory for the `batch_mod`/`file_glob` branches queued next.

- **`DIR` fixes for two hardware-found regressions from the 2026-07-26 date/time-first column reorder — IMPLEMENTED AND BUILD-VERIFIED 2026-07-26, NOT YET HARDWARE-TESTED. Pure userland (`progs/dir.asm`), zero kernel changes.** Both found from the same hardware round (transcript `gt2.txt`), reported alongside an actual-vs-desired output comparison.
  - **Bug 1: missing separator between the size/`<DIR>` field and the name.** The reorder moved the size-or-`<DIR>` field to sit immediately before the name but never added a print between them — a file's name printed glued directly onto its own size digits (e.g. `805unset.c`, no space at all), while a directory only got the one space already baked into `dir_tag`'s own trailing `" <DIR> "` text (inconsistent with itself, and both inconsistent with every other column pair in this same listing, which all use a 2-space separator). Fixed by adding an explicit `"  "` (2-space) `K_INMSG` print at the shared `pde_print_name` label, immediately before the name is printed via `K_MSG` — the single point both the file and directory paths already funnel through, so one fix covers both.
  - **Bug 2: 24-hour time instead of the requested 12-hour AM/PM format.** The user's own desired-output example showed times like `09:00 PM`. Added a conversion right after `wr_hour` (the existing 24-hour value) is stored: computes a new `wr_hour12` (1-12) and a 3-byte `dir_ampm` string (`"AM"`/`"PM"` + NUL) via a plain `smi 12`/`lbnf` branch (hour < 12 → AM, hour unchanged, except 0→12; hour >= 12 → PM, hour-12, except a resulting 0→12) — hand-traced against all the interesting boundary cases (0→12AM, 9→9AM, 12→12PM, 13→1PM, 23→11PM) before trusting it. The print sequence now prints `wr_hour12` (not the raw `wr_hour`) for the hour digits, followed by `" "` + `dir_ampm` after the minute field, and the date/time-to-size separator was widened from 1 space to 2 (matching the newly-consistent 2-space convention established by Bug 1's own fix).
  - **Verified**: clean assemble (0 errors/warnings), D-clobber sweep, self-overwrite sweep, no duplicate labels — all clean. Full clean `make`+`make progs`+`make test` (kernel untouched — pure `progs/` change, 31/8 `bin`/`test/bin` present). **Not yet hardware-tested** — needs a round confirming: the name now has a clean 2-space gap from the preceding field for both files and directories; AM/PM display is correct across a range of real file timestamps (not just the hand-traced boundary cases); and a general regression check of `DIR`'s own column alignment now that the separator width changed in two places.

- **`COPY`/glob-expansion root cause found and fixed: `ARGV_MAX_ARGS=16` silently truncates a wildcard expansion that produces too many matches, dropping trailing arguments (including the destination) with no warning — IMPLEMENTED AND BUILD-VERIFIED 2026-07-26, NOT YET HARDWARE-TESTED. Pure userland (`progs/shell.asm`), zero kernel changes.** This is the real root cause of the `"copy C:/cfg/*.* ."` failure reported earlier the same day (`"Destination must be an existing directory for multiple sources."`), which had initially been suspected as a cross-drive or `K_PATH_RESOLVE` issue in `COPY` itself.
  - **How it was actually found**: a temporary diagnostic added to `progs/copy.asm`'s `check_dst_is_dir` (printing `K_PATH_RESOLVE`'s own DF/cluster/final-component result, plus the directory-scan outcome) was re-run per the user's request and its output showed `check_dst_is_dir` being asked to resolve `dupefile13.txt` — a *source* file from the glob-expanded `C:/cfg/*.*`, not `.`, the real intended destination. This proved the bug was upstream of `COPY` entirely: the shell's own glob-expansion code was silently handing `COPY` a corrupted argument list.
  - **Root cause**: `progs/shell.asm`'s `ge_append_tmp` (part of `glob_expand`, which builds the final argv table from wildcard matches) has always silently dropped any entry once `RUN_ARGV_TABLE` reaches its hard `ARGV_MAX_ARGS=16` capacity, with no warning of any kind. `/cfg` on the user's test card has more than enough matching files to fill all 16 argv slots on its own — so the trailing, non-wildcard `.` (the actual destination argument, typed last on the command line) got silently dropped along with any excess matches, leaving `COPY` with an argv list made entirely of source files and no destination at all — which then misresolved the *last surviving source* as if it were the destination, exactly matching the diagnostic's own output.
  - **Fixed** by adding a new `ge_truncated` flag (`progs/shell.asm`): initialized to 0 alongside the existing `ge_budget_exhausted` flag at the top of `glob_expand`; set to 1 by `ge_append_tmp` at the exact point it would otherwise have silently dropped an entry (restructured from `lbdf gat_done` to `lbnf gat_not_full` so the capacity-exceeded case falls through to setting the flag before still doing the same drop-and-continue as before — the *drop* behavior is unchanged, only now it's observable). `ge_expand_done` checks the flag before ever publishing the (now known-incomplete) argv table: if set, prints `"Too many matches (max 16 arguments including the command and destination) -- command not run.",13,10` and aborts the whole line (`lbr start`) instead of running a command against silently-corrupted arguments — a visible, safe failure in place of the old silent-and-misleading one.
  - **The now-unneeded `check_dst_is_dir` diagnostic was removed** from `progs/copy.asm`, restoring it to its original, clean form (plain `K_PATH_RESOLVE` + directory scan, no diagnostic prints, no `cdd_diag_*` scratch fields) — it served its purpose (finding this bug) and the real fix lives in the shell's glob expansion, not in `COPY` itself.
  - **Verified**: clean assemble on all three touched points (0 errors/warnings), D-clobber sweep, self-overwrite sweep (4 hits, all confirmed pre-existing/outside the new code's own line ranges), no duplicate labels. Full clean `make`+`make progs`+`make test` (kernel untouched — pure `progs/` change, 31/8 `bin`/`test/bin` present). **Not yet hardware-tested** — needs a round confirming: the original failing case (`copy C:/cfg/*.* .` from a directory with >16 matching entries) now prints the new "Too many matches" error and does not run any copy, instead of silently misbehaving; an ordinary wildcard copy well under the 16-argument cap still works exactly as before (regression check); and ideally a case landing exactly at 16 total arguments (command + matches + destination) to confirm the boundary itself doesn't false-positive.

- **Housekeeping (2026-07-27 to 2026-07-29), no functional changes worth their own bullet**: repo file permission bits reset on `Makefile`/`boot/*.asm`/`kernel/*.asm` (content byte-identical, `a5c8b56`); two small new userland utilities, `BAUD <rate>` (`progs/baud.asm`, a table-driven lookup of the 8 standard rates 300-57600 into `f_usetbd`) and `CLS` (`progs/cls.asm`, a two-instruction `ESC[H ESC[J` ANSI clear) (`21223df`); the legacy, long-superseded C-source `getenv.c`/`setenv.c`/`unsetenv.c` and a stale prebuilt `sys/elfdos-sys` binary deleted (fully replaced by `lib/env.asm`'s own assembly implementation and the real `sys/sys.c`-built installer), plus a new `linuxify.bat` added (`1d7e63a`).

- **`TOUCH`/`LABEL` commands, an EDLIN FreeDOS-parity pass, and `YR`/`YS` YMODEM-CRC batch transfer — IMPLEMENTED 2026-07-30 (`fdccc3c`). `TOUCH`/`LABEL`/DIR's header fix are HARDWARE-CONFIRMED; the EDLIN parity pass and `YR`/`YS` were build-verified only at this point** (see the `a26679a` entry below for EDLIN's own subsequent hardware round).
  - **`TOUCH <path>`** (wildcard-aware via the already-existing `lib/file_glob.asm`): new kernel primitive `K_FILE_TOUCH` refreshes a file's last-write date/time to "now" without touching its content; `progs/touch.asm` is a thin wrapper.
  - **`LABEL`**: implemented entirely in userland (`lib/vollabel.asm`), not kernel-resident — an initial kernel-resident design was tried first and reverted because it pushed boot headroom negative. The shipped version does raw `K_SECREAD`/`K_SECWRITE` sector I/O directly against the boot-populated `BPB_DATA_PTR`, keeping the volume-label directory entry and the boot sector's own copy of the label in sync (`_vol_sync_bootsector`) — added specifically because `fsck.vfat` flagged a root-directory/boot-sector label mismatch once `LABEL` could change one without the other.
  - **`DIR` gained an MS-DOS-style `"Volume in drive X is <label>"` / `"Directory of <path>"` header**, via new `lib/pathstr.asm` (the cluster-to-path walk extracted out of `pwd.asm`, which shrank to a thin wrapper around the shared routine).
  - **Two real bugs fixed in the same commit**: a `DIR` regression (`RD` not reloaded after the new volume-header code ran, corrupting whatever `RD` held for the rest of the listing — see the follow-on `5a2d463` entry below for further header bugs found afterward) and a `LABEL` argv-parsing bug that silently discarded extra arguments.
  - **EDLIN FreeDOS-parity**: extended line-reference syntax (`.`/`$`/`#`/`+n`/`-n`, not just a bare number); `L`/`P` fixed to match real FreeDOS behavior (closes an open concern from the earlier v1.1 pass); new `T`(ransfer)/`R`(eplace)/`C`(opy)/`M`(ove) commands; `ed_parse_range` widened from 2 to 4 slots with real `,,N`-style skip-comma support. Escape sequences inside `R`/`S` search-and-replace text, and the `?`-confirm flag beyond `S`, are deliberately still deferred. Also swept `edlin.asm`'s small-constant `ADD16`/`SUB16` sites over to `INC`/`DEC`, per this project's standing size-reduction convention.
  - **`YR`/`YS`**: YMODEM-CRC batch send/receive, the YMODEM-protocol companions to the already-existing XMODEM-based `MR`/`MS` pair. New `lib/ymodem.asm` (CRC16, 32-bit decimal format/parse, mode-aware direct-BIOS I/O with the same `-u`/`-b` flags `MR`/`MS` use, polled timeout, block-transfer loops mirroring `MR`/`MS`'s own already-proven shape) plus `progs/yr.asm` (receive: batch loop over multiple incoming files, size-truncated writes, Y/N overwrite prompt) and `progs/ys.asm` (send: glob-expanded multi-file, a two-tier local-vs-protocol-fatal error distinction, `$1A` padding on the final block). Zero kernel changes for this whole piece.
  - **Verification**: all three pieces build clean — clean assembles, full D-clobber/self-overwrite/duplicate-label/gotcha-18 sweeps, correct `'EDF'` magic and `Lowest address` on every affected binary.

- **Loadable batch module Phase 1 (fixed address) + `PROG_BASE`/`KERN_LOAD` moved $4600→$4200 — CONFIRMED WORKING ON HARDWARE 2026-07-30 (`fb49917`).**
  - `kernel/batch.asm`'s `batch_start`/`batch_readline`/`batch_goto` — previously permanently kernel-resident even though only ever used while a `.bat` script is actually running — moved out into a new, separately-built `kernel/batch_mod.asm`, landing on disk as `/bin/batch.mod` and reloaded fresh into RAM on every `K_BATCH_START` call, at a **fixed**, compile-time address (`BATCHMOD_BASE = $D000`, `include/batchmod.inc`).
  - **Deliberately narrow Phase 1 scope**: every internal branch/data reference in the module is a plain absolute address — no relocation, no RB-relative addressing. This only works on a machine with real RAM above `$D000` (the developer's own 1802/Mini, 60K RAM) — explicitly **not for release** as-is; Phase 2 (below) is the real-relocation follow-up, designed the very next day.
  - `kernel/batch.asm` itself became a dispatcher+loader: `K_BATCH_START`/`READLINE`/`GOTO` keep their exact names/addresses/contracts, but now check a new kernel-resident "batch active" flag first, fast-pathing to `DF=1` with the module never touched when no batch is running (`K_BATCH_READLINE` runs on every single shell command cycle, so this path has to stay cheap). `batch_mod_load` reloads the module via `kernel/loader.asm`'s already-idle `prog_fcb`/`prog_iobuf` (reused, not a second dedicated FCB+iobuf pair — a first draft added its own 683-byte pair and was caught/fixed before reaching hardware, since it would have made the kernel *larger*, not smaller). `mem_top` is clamped to `BATCHMOD_MEMTOP_CLAMP` (`$CFFF`) for the module's residency and restored once the batch ends; the reservation is refused cleanly (nothing changed) rather than partially granted if the module wouldn't fully fit under the *current* `mem_top` — anything already claimed there by an active glob/redirect/`%N` reservation for the very command line invoking the `.bat` file must not be clobbered. The "batch active" flag alone gates nesting, since the module's own in-RAM state can't be trusted to reflect "no batch running" even when apparently clean (a short/partial disk read wouldn't necessarily zero every reserved byte).
  - Net kernel-margin recovery: `Highest address` `$42bd`→`$409f` (542 bytes). `PROG_BASE`/`KERN_LOAD` moved `$4600`→`$4200` as a result — short of the originally-discussed `$4000` (would have needed ~160 more bytes nobody could find safely; moving `TOUCH` itself kernel-resident was considered and rejected, since — unlike `LABEL` — it depends on `dir_last_off`/`dir_cur_lba`, transient kernel-internal state with no userland-visible equivalent, so it would cost more than it would save). `$4200` leaves 1K more program RAM than the old `$4600` while keeping 353 bytes of real kernel margin. `boot/mbr.asm`/`boot/krnboot.asm` moved in lockstep.
  - **First hardware round (2026-07-30): CONFIRMED WORKING** — boots cleanly at the new `PROG_BASE`; 8 ordinary commands exercised (`dir`/`ls`/`cd`/`md`/`rd`/drive switch/`type`/`copy`); the existing `IF`/`GOTO` and `%0`-`%9`/`%ERRORLEVEL` batch test scripts both re-passed, exercising the module loader and the `mem_top` clamp/restore lifecycle across two sequential batch runs. Left unexercised this round: `/autoexec.bat`'s own boot-time call site (deliberately, to isolate boot success from the batch mechanism itself), and the two defensive/error paths (bounds-check refusal, nested-batch rejection).

- **EDLIN hardware bug fixes + a shared `lib/lineedit.asm` line-editing library — CONFIRMED WORKING ON HARDWARE 2026-07-31 (`a26679a`).**
  - **Seven real bugs found via hardware testing, all fixed**: relative line-number references (`+n`/`-n`) landing on the wrong line due to a register clobbered across a call (this project's own recurring gotcha #10 pattern); `P`(age) never actually pausing; no way to open EDLIN with an empty buffer; `W`/`E` not accepting a filename argument; `R`'s range excluding its own last line; `L`'s default lookback overshooting past the intended 11 lines. `W`/`E` were also redesigned to share one save/exit routine, each now taking an optional filename argument, per the user's own spec.
  - **`lib/lineedit.asm`** (845 lines, new): extracts the shell's own line-editing (arrow keys, Emacs/readline Ctrl shortcuts, in-place insert/delete — originally built directly into `progs/shell.asm`'s `read_line_with_history`, see the command-line-editing history above) into a standalone, linkable `K_INPUTL` replacement any program can use — built specifically so EDLIN's own text entry gets the same editing behavior the shell already has, without a second, duplicated copy of that logic.

- **Loadable batch module Phase 2 (genuine relocation) + general-purpose `K_HIMEM_RESERVE`/`RELEASE` + Link/02 `-m` module mode — CONFIRMED WORKING ON HARDWARE 2026-07-31 (`254e4bd`).**
  - Phase 1 (above) only worked on a machine with real RAM at a hardcoded `$D000`. Phase 2 makes the module genuinely relocatable: **Link/02 gained a new `-m` (module) output mode** that emits a fixup table alongside the linked code (a new capability in the shared toolchain, not just this project); `lib/modload.asm` (new, 495 lines) applies that fixup table at load time via the kernel's new, general-purpose `K_HIMEM_RESERVE`/`K_HIMEM_RELEASE` (exposing the kernel's own already-hardware-proven `_himem_reserve`/`_himem_release`, `kernel/redir.asm`, to userland/other kernel code for the first time) to place the module wherever it actually fits, rather than at a fixed address. `lib/icall.asm` (new, 161 lines) dispatches into the loaded module's own entry points, since the target address is now only known at runtime.
  - **Two real bugs found via hardware testing** (the kernel booted, printed its banner, then hung on *every* boot, since `/autoexec.bat` processing runs unconditionally through this same new code path): (1) `icall` tried to load the runtime jump target into `R3` via two separate `PHI`/`PLO` instructions while `R3` was still the active P register — corrupting the very next instruction fetch before the second half of the address was even written; fixed by assembling the target in `R3` while executing under a *different* register (`R7`), matching the exact technique `scall.asm`'s own `call`/`callbr` trampoline (the shared SCRT helper at the project root) already uses for this same reason. (2) `batch_mod_load` called `mod_load` with only the path set, never the required FCB/iobuf arguments — `mod_load`'s first real action (`K_FILE_OPEN`) ran against whatever garbage was already sitting in those registers.
  - `PROG_BASE`/`KERN_LOAD` moved `$4200`→`$4400` to restore the kernel margin this retrofit ate into — this is the value still current as of 2026-08-01.
  - **Confirmed working on hardware**: full test-script suite plus ordinary commands, after both fixes above.

- **`DIR`'s volume/`"Directory of <path>"` headers fixed: wrong drive, missing cases, multi-argument listings — CONFIRMED WORKING ON HARDWARE ACROSS SEVERAL ROUNDS 2026-07-31 (`5a2d463`).**
  - **Wrong-drive bug**: a path argument on a drive other than the active one showed either no header at all, or the *active* drive's own label/path instead of the one actually being listed — `path_print_from_cluster` and the volume-label lookup both always read `cur_drive`, never the drive a `"X:/..."` argument actually resolved to (which `K_PATH_RESOLVE` switches the active BPB to, without ever touching `cur_drive` itself — the same active-BPB-vs-`cur_drive` distinction this project has hit before, e.g. the `K_GETCURDIR`/XCOPY cross-drive fixes). `path_print_from_cluster` now takes an explicit drive override; the volume-label lookup already reads whichever BPB is active, so activating the right one first was sufficient there.
  - **A glob-matched listing** (`dir C:/*.dat`) previously showed no header at all — fixed.
  - **A multi-argument `DIR`** (`dir C:/foo D:/bar`) previously showed only a one-line summary per directory argument and printed `cur_drive`'s own volume label once for the whole command, regardless of what was actually being listed. Each literal directory argument (and each glob-pattern argument) now gets the full treatment — both headers plus a complete listing, equivalent to running `DIR` on that argument alone — separated by a blank line between consecutive listings; the single top-level volume header is skipped entirely for this case, since each argument now prints its own. File arguments and glob-matched files are unaffected (still one line each).
  - Required promoting three pieces of what used to be single-use, end-of-program code into real subroutines (`dir_resolve_and_classify`, `dir_list_entries`, `dir_print_volume_header`) so the multi-argument loop could invoke each once per argument instead of once per program run. `lib/file_glob.asm` gained a small `glob_get_dir` accessor so a glob's own already-resolved prefix directory could be reused for its header without reaching into its otherwise-private context layout.
  - **Confirmed working on hardware across several rounds**, including fixing the duplicate-header and missing-blank-line regressions those very rounds found along the way.

- **Current baseline as of 2026-08-01 (catch-up documentation pass)**: `main` is clean, up to date with `origin/main`, everything above merged and hardware-confirmed except where noted. `PROG_BASE`/`KERN_LOAD` = `$4400`. An old, unrelated experimental branch, `fcb-vtable-experiment` (FCB_KIND field + NUL-kind dispatch, 3 commits), diverged 2026-07-17 and has not been touched or rebased since 2026-07-19 — predates essentially everything in this section and would need reassessment against current `main` before reviving.

- **Planned next steps, as of 2026-08-01 (recovered directly from the user — the original session notes for these were never committed to CLAUDE.md or saved anywhere else, and could not be found)**:
  1. **DONE (2026-08-02).** A kernel/EDLIN/LS size-reduction pass, run via a Fable 5 subagent in an isolated git worktree (`.claude/worktrees/redir-consolidation`, branch `redir-consolidation-agent`) — 7 commits, **CONFIRMED WORKING ON HARDWARE and merged to `main`**. Immediately followed by a related, separately-tracked change: `PROG_BASE` decoupled from `KERN_LOAD` and moved to `$4300` (giving every program 256 more bytes of usable RAM) — also **CONFIRMED WORKING ON HARDWARE**, one commit ahead of `main` in the same worktree, pending its own merge. See the dedicated entries below for both.
  2. **A `CHKDSK`-style utility.** Check-only mode **implemented and fully build-verified 2026-08-01, next up for its own first hardware round** — see its own detailed entry below for the full design and verification record. `-f` fix mode remains a separate, not-yet-started follow-up (the reboot-after-fix design conclusion still stands).
  3. **`YR`/`YS` (YMODEM-CRC batch transfer, added 2026-07-30, `fdccc3c`) don't actually work** — shipped as build-verified only, and hardware testing since has found them broken. No failure-mode detail captured yet; needs the user's own repro/symptom before diagnosing, per this project's standing hardware-only-testing workflow.
  4. **A User's Guide (day-to-day ELF-DOS use) and a Developer's Guide (program structure, kernel API, etc.).** Not started. Likely the lowest-urgency of the four (no bugs, no blocked features) — a good candidate for whenever the other three are quiet.

- **`CHKDSK` (check-only mode) — IMPLEMENTED AND FULLY BUILD-VERIFIED 2026-08-01, NOT YET HARDWARE-TESTED.** New `progs/chkdsk.asm` (~950 lines, the largest single-file addition this session, comparable in scope to EDLIN/XCOPY), linked against the already-proven `lib/fmt32.asm` for its summary report's byte counts (same pattern `bin/dir`/`bin/stat` already use). **Zero kernel changes** — every primitive needed (`K_DIR_OPEN`/`READ`/`SAVE_STATE`/`RESTORE_STATE`, `K_SECREAD`, `K_PATH_RESOLVE`, `K_GETCURDIR`, `BPB_DATA_PTR`) already existed, so this carries zero risk to the kernel's own tight `PROG_BASE` margin. Full design at `~/.claude/plans/graceful-fluttering-summit.md`. `-f` fix mode deliberately out of scope for this pass (see the roadmap entry above) — this is `CHKDSK [X:]`, check-and-report only, no on-disk writes anywhere in the file.
  - **Drive selection**: no argument checks the active drive (`K_GETCURDIR`, no `K_PATH_RESOLVE` needed at all in this branch, since the active drive's BPB is already the right one); an explicit `"X:"` argument activates that drive's BPB via the same `K_PATH_RESOLVE`-purely-for-its-`_switch_drive`-side-effect trick already proven twice in this codebase (`xc_walk` via `K_STAT`, `glob_next` via `K_PATH_RESOLVE`) — `cur_drive` itself is never touched. Once activated, CHKDSK's own architecture guarantees the active drive can never change again mid-run, since every primitive it uses after that point (`K_DIR_OPEN`/`READ`/`SAVE_STATE`/`RESTORE_STATE`/`K_SECREAD`) is confirmed, via a repo-wide grep of `_switch_drive`'s every caller, to never itself call `_switch_drive`.
  - **Recursive tree walk, `chk_walk_dir`**: chosen to build on `K_DIR_SAVE_STATE`/`K_DIR_RESTORE_STATE` (2026-07-27) rather than `xcopy.asm`'s older `heap_bump` collect-then-recurse pattern — CHKDSK never needs to reorder entries (unlike `LS`) or build destination paths (unlike `XCOPY`/`COPY`), only "resume this level's scan after the subtree beneath one entry finishes," exactly what those two calls were built for, with a much smaller memory footprint than collecting whole directories into RAM first. Per-recursion-level state (this level's own cluster, its parent's cluster, its own depth, and its own 9-byte `K_DIR_SAVE_STATE` buffer) lives in a fixed `chk_frames` array (`CHK_MAX_DEPTH=24` × `CHK_FRAME_LEN=14` bytes = 336 bytes total) indexed by recursion depth — not a second dynamic allocator, not the hardware stack, both considered and rejected as more complex than needed for a handful of small, easily depth-indexed fields. The array slot *currently* active is tracked via a shared global, `chk_frame_ptr`, reloaded fresh via a tiny `chk_get_frame` helper before every single use and explicitly protected (push/pop, with an immediate resync of the global after popping) only across the one recursive call itself — the exact `xcw_frame_ptr` pattern `progs/xcopy.asm` already proved correct on hardware, needed here because the recursive call's own execution otherwise leaves the global pointing at its own, now-dead frame. A hard depth cap (`CHK_MAX_DEPTH`) defends against a corrupt on-disk `..`-cycle turning into unbounded recursion — hitting it prints a message and simply stops recursing at that one branch, never treated as fatal to the whole scan.
  - **Per-file check, `chk_check_file`/`chk_walk_chain`**: independently walks each file's own FAT16 cluster chain via raw `K_SECREAD` calls (`chk_fat_read`, `chk_cluster_to_lba` — userland reimplementations of kernel-internal `fat_get`/`_cluster_to_lba`, hand-traced against those real, already-proven routines rather than re-derived from prose, specifically to avoid repeating `_cluster_to_lba`'s own historical `RC`-clobber bug, gotcha #10's "worst instance"). Bounded by `max_clust+1` hops (a chain cannot legitimately need more), defending against a corrupt self-looping chain. Marks every visited cluster in a "seen" bitmap (`chk_bitmap`, 1 bit/cluster, 8KB static allocation sized for FAT16's worst case but only zeroed up to `(max_clust+8)>>3` — the real volume's own actual size) — a cluster already marked when visited a second time is a cross-link, reported immediately without stopping that chain's own walk. **No division needed, and — after a design correction mid-session — no subtraction needed either**: `chk_walk_chain` hands back both the chain's total byte-capacity *and* its value as of one hop earlier (`prev_capacity`, snapshotted for free before each hop's own addition), which is exactly the "smallest size this many clusters could hold" bound the size-vs-chain comparison needs, with zero new arithmetic at the call site. A 0-byte file with exactly one allocated cluster is explicitly tolerated (this project's own lazy-first-cluster-allocation convention from `file_open`'s history) — more than one cluster on a 0-byte file is not.
  - **Directory structural validation, pass B (`chk_scan_dir_raw`)**: a fully separate, second raw-sector walk of each directory (root's fixed area, or a subdirectory's own cluster chain via the same `chk_cluster_to_lba`/`chk_fat_read` primitives) — needed because `dir_read`'s own LFN resolution *silently hides* exactly what this check needs to see (a checksum mismatch falls back to the plain 8.3 name rather than surfacing it, `kernel/dir.asm:374-389`). Detects duplicate short names (an 11-byte raw compare against every other real entry already seen in the same directory, tracked via a running byte-offset rather than an index×11 multiply — `chk_dup_next_offset` doubles as both "how many real bytes of data exist so far" and "where to append next," eliminating the only multiply this piece of the design would otherwise have needed) and orphaned/checksum-mismatched LFN runs, via `chk_dir_chksum` — a byte-for-byte mirror of `kernel/dir.asm`'s own already-proven `_dir_chksum`, including preserving its own documented bug-fix note about why the running checksum must live in `RB.0` across the loop-counter check rather than `D`. Bounded to `CHK_DUPNAME_CAP=256` entries per directory (2816-byte static buffer, reused per directory level, not per recursion depth); an oversized directory gets one "truncated" message, not one per further entry.
  - **FAT-table second pass, `chk_fat_scan_lost`**: runs once after the whole tree walk completes, a sequential scan of every FAT sector (reusing `lib/vollabel.asm`'s own proven 24-bit sequential-LBA-increment shape, `chk_lba_inc`) — any cluster whose FAT entry is nonzero (allocated) but whose "seen" bit was never set by the tree walk is a lost cluster; a zero entry tallies toward the summary's free-space count. One pass covers both tallies.
  - **Summary report**: DOS-style, adapted to what this program actually tracks (total/used/free space via `fmt_size32`, file/directory/hidden counts, and per-problem-category tallies that only print a line when nonzero, matching real `CHKDSK`'s own "only mention it if it happened" convention). The one genuinely new arithmetic routine in the whole file, `chk_mul16x8` (16-bit × 8-bit → 32-bit, standard shift-add, needed for `total_bytes = max_clust * spc * 512`), plus a general 32-bit-value-±-32-bit-value pair (`chk_add32`/`chk_sub32`) used throughout. A defensive overflow check follows the final ×512 scaling step (checking the last shift's own carry-out) — unreachable by any real FAT16 volume this project has ever tested against, but cheap insurance rather than an assumed-safe silent wraparound.
  - **A real, load-bearing design correction caught during plan review, before any code was written**: the first draft of the plan described a "32-bit sub16-pair idiom" for the summary's `used_bytes = total_bytes - free_bytes` and for accumulating byte-capacity during the chain walk — the user directly caught that this is wrong: `ADD16`/`SUB16` each perform one 16-bit-register-pair operation in a single shot, and calling one on the low word plus a second, independent one on the high word does **not** propagate carry/borrow across the 16-bit boundary correctly. Investigated the codebase's own real precedent (`kernel/file.asm`'s `fread_check_eof`, "full 32-bit subtract-with-borrow") and found the actual proven idiom: 4 individual byte-level `SM`/`SMB` steps, LSB first, each subtrahend byte staged via `str r2` immediately before its own consumer (gotcha #18). `CHK_SUB32` copies this exactly; `CHK_ADD32` mirrors it with `ADD`/`ADC` in place of `SM`/`SMB` (no existing 32-bit-add precedent existed in this codebase to copy directly, but the byte-chain shape is identical). Both were independently verified via a byte-accurate mechanical simulation — not just Python's own `+`/`-`, which would trivially "pass" even a broken instruction sequence — across 200,000+ random 32-bit pairs plus every carry/borrow-propagation boundary, before any assembly was written. `chk_mul16x8` was verified the same way, separately, across 200,000+ `(a,b)` pairs including `spc=128` (the largest value confirmed on this project's own real hardware) and the theoretical maximum.
  - **Two real bugs found during implementation, both caught before this ever reached hardware**:
    1. **Gotcha #4** (D-clobber): the LFN-checksum-stash code in `chk_scan_dir_raw` did `ldn rf` (load the checksum byte into D) immediately followed by `mov r8, chk_pb_pending_chksum` (which clobbers D) before the `str` that was meant to save it — caught by the standard automated D-clobber sweep, fixed by reordering the `mov` before the `ldn`.
    2. **A more serious one, caught only by a final manual re-read, not by any sweep**: the "seen" cluster bitmap (`chk_bitmap`) was never zero-initialized anywhere in the program. `ds` does **not** guarantee zeroed memory in this codebase (confirmed via `progs/xcopy.asm`'s own established "explicitly zero every counter at start" convention, followed everywhere else in this file for its own tally counters) — without an explicit zero loop, every single run would have started from whatever garbage happened to be sitting in RAM, producing false cross-link reports and wrong lost-cluster detection from the very first cluster marked. Fixed by adding an explicit zero loop, sized to the real `(max_clust+8)>>3` needed-bytes count (computed from the just-read `BPBBLK_MAX_CLUST`), inserted right after `chk_read_bpb` and before the tree walk begins.
  - **Verification**: clean assemble at every implementation stage (10 stages total, each independently assembled and swept before moving to the next — skeleton/args, `chk_cluster_to_lba`/`chk_fat_read`, `CHK_ADD32`/`CHK_SUB32`, `chk_walk_chain`+bitmap, the recursive tree walk, the per-file check, pass B, the FAT second pass, the multiply+summary, and a final control-flow re-read). D-clobber/self-overwrite/gotcha-18/duplicate-label sweeps run after every stage — every hit traced by hand to either a confirmed fix (the two real bugs above) or a confirmed false positive (the sweep's own known limitation: `lbdf`/`lbnf`/a bare `mov` genuinely don't depend on `D`, only `DF`, which `sub16`/`chk_scale_mul_result_x512`'s own final `smb`/`shlc` correctly set as their real output — the same false-positive class already documented elsewhere in this file). No `proc`/`endp` in this file at all (confirmed 0/0) — like every other `progs/*.asm`, it's flat, so gotcha #20 (the bare-top-level-label-between-procs bug) structurally cannot apply here. Full clean `make`+`make progs`+`make test`: kernel confirmed byte-unchanged (`Highest address` still `$423E`), `bin/chkdsk` present with correct `'EDF'` magic and `Lowest address` matching `PROG_BASE` (`$4400`) — the cheap tell this project's own gotcha #20 entry recommends checking on every `link02` invocation, re-confirmed here even though it structurally can't apply to a flat file, just as a matter of discipline. Final control-flow re-read specifically checked that every "report a problem" path *continues* scanning and tallying rather than silently `rtn`-ing out early (grepped every `rtn` in the file and traced each one back to a legitimate routine-exit point, none hiding mid-loop where a `lbr ..._done`/`lbr ..._next` was intended instead) — the exact class of mistake this project's own `mr.asm`/`ms.asm`/argv-tokenizer history already warns is easy to introduce during exactly this kind of multi-branch restructuring.
  - **Not achievable in this environment, flagged honestly**: no hardware or emulator access exists, so nothing above has been exercised against a real (or deliberately corrupted) FAT16 image. Needs a careful first hardware round given the size/risk of this feature (comparable to EDLIN/XCOPY): a clean volume (confirm no false positives at all); a file with a deliberately truncated cluster chain (the exact signature of this project's own real >64K bug history, `"cluster chain length is N bytes, truncating"` in `fsck.fat`'s own terms); a directory with duplicate short names; and ideally a large-`spc` card, since that's precisely the configuration that has broken multiple *other* pieces of this codebase before (the >64K bug hunt itself, `_cluster_to_lba`'s own `RC`-clobber bug) and is exactly the class of edge case this feature's own arithmetic was built to handle correctly.
  - **⭐ ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED (2026-08-02) — the first real hardware round found widespread apparent false positives, not the clean pass hoped for.** The user ran `CHKDSK` against two partitions (`E:` and `C:`) that `fsck.fat` had *just* independently confirmed clean, with no deliberate corruption injected yet (`test/corrupt.asm` untested at this point). Both runs reported: a `"Cluster chain too long (possible cycle), stopped."` for **every single file and non-root directory on the volume** (23/23 on `E:`, 158/158 on `C:` — 100% failure rate, zero successful chain-walks anywhere), and a `"Lost cluster: N"` list covering nearly every real allocated cluster (43 on `E:`, 186 on `C:`), with only a handful of small gaps in the low cluster-number range. Full transcript in `~/claude_io/chk1.txt`.
    - **Exhaustive static re-verification found no bug in any of the obvious suspects**, each checked line-by-line against its real, already-hardware-proven kernel counterpart or its own declared offsets: `chk_fat_read`'s FAT sector/byte-offset arithmetic (cross-checked directly against `kernel/fat.asm`'s `_fat_load_sector`/`fat_get`, byte-for-byte, including the exact "high byte = sector index, low byte ×2 = byte offset" convention — matches); `chk_cluster_to_lba` (matches `kernel/dir.asm`'s `_cluster_to_lba` shift/add shape); `chk_read_bpb`'s `BPBBLK_*` offsets (all 8 fields hand-verified against `kernel_api.inc`'s own equ constants AND against `kernel/kernel.asm`'s real `_kernel_data` proc layout, confirming every field lands at its documented offset with no gaps); `chk_mark_cluster`'s bit-index math; `chk_walk_chain`'s "is remaining == 0" 16-bit zero-check and its per-iteration decrement (both correct); the full static data section for any declared-size/actual-usage mismatch or accidental overlap (none found — one linear, non-overlapping sequence of `ds` declarations, single `org` at the top of the file). Control flow was also traced end to end: `chk_read_bpb` genuinely runs (from `chk_print_header`) *before* the bitmap-zero-init code that reads `chk_max_clust`, for BOTH the with-argument (`chkdsk E:`) and no-argument (`chkdsk`, checks the already-active drive with no switch needed at all) paths — ruling out drive-activation timing and ruling out the initially-suspected "read chk_max_clust before chk_read_bpb populates it" theory from a prior session.
    - **The numbers themselves argue for a specific, narrow hypothesis**: a 100% failure rate with zero cross-linked-cluster reports anywhere is inconsistent with a *large* but wrong `remaining` bound (a real ~8192-cluster-sized volume — this partition size matches this project's own previously-confirmed `spc=128` cards — walking through garbage-but-plausible FAT values would, by ordinary birthday-paradox reasoning, be expected to re-visit an already-marked cluster (triggering "Cross-linked cluster") within roughly `1.25*sqrt(8192)≈113` steps on average, long before exhausting an ~8192-iteration bound; it's also inconsistent with the plausible real-world runtime of a normal interactive test session, since 8192 real `K_SECREAD` calls × ~181 total chain-walks would take unrealistically long on real hardware). Both the *absence* of cross-link reports and the *small number* of clusters that ever get marked "seen" (only 7 distinct clusters across all 158 calls on `C:`) point at `chk_wc_remaining` starting out far too small on essentially every call, not a large-but-wrong bound. No definitive root cause was found by static reading alone despite this narrowing.
    - **Three `; TEMPORARY DIAGNOSTIC` print blocks added** (`progs/chkdsk.asm`), matching this project's own standing practice of instrumenting rather than continuing to guess once static analysis stalls: (1) in `chk_print_header`, right after `call chk_read_bpb`, prints `chk_max_clust`/`chk_spc`/`chk_spc_shift`'s real values once per program run — directly confirms or refutes whether the BPB read itself produces the expected ~8191/128/7-ish values; (2) in `chk_walk_chain`, right after `chk_wc_cluster` is stashed (a safe insertion point — nothing downstream depends on any register surviving across it, `cwc_loop` reloads everything fresh from memory by design), prints the starting cluster and the initial `chk_wc_remaining` bound for *every* call — directly tests whether the bound is genuinely large or small, and whether it's consistent across calls; (3) at `cwc_toolong`, prints `chk_wc_hops` (how many clusters were actually processed before the bound was hit) for every failing call. All three read their values fresh from memory (never from a register that might have been clobbered by an intervening call), matching this project's own established diagnostic discipline.
    - **Verified**: clean assemble (0 errors/warnings, 2253 lines/5897 bytes generated), D-clobber sweep (script-based, checking every `mov`/`add16`/`sub16`/`shl16`/etc. immediately followed by a D-dependent consumer with no refresh in between) — 0 hits across the whole file including the new diagnostic code. No duplicate labels. Full `make bin/chkdsk` rebuild clean, `Lowest address: 4300` matching `PROG_BASE`, `'EDF'` magic confirmed via direct hex dump. Kernel and every other binary untouched (`git status` shows only `progs/chkdsk.asm` modified).
    - **Diagnostic result (2026-08-02, `chk2.txt`) — fully decisive**: `[DIAG maxclust=65535 spc=16 spcshift=4]` — `max_clust` reads as `$FFFF`, the maximum representable 16-bit value, entirely legitimate for this specific card (a near-maximal FAT16 volume: `536,862,720` bytes / `spc=16` (8KB clusters) ≈ 65535 clusters, right at FAT16's own practical ceiling — a genuinely different, smaller-`spc` card than the `spc=128` cards this project has tested against before, and the first time this project's own CHKDSK-style arithmetic has been exercised at this specific boundary). Every single one of the 158 `chk_walk_chain` calls printed `[WC start=<N> rem=0]` — `chk_wc_remaining` was `0` from the very first read, on literally every call, and every one immediately hit `[WC toolong hops=0]` — zero clusters ever visited or marked, on any call, matching the transcript's own summary counts exactly (133 files/25 dirs/186 lost clusters, identical to the original `chk1.txt` report).
    - **Root cause**: `chk_walk_chain`'s own bound computation — `mov rf, chk_max_clust / lda rf / phi r9 / ldn rf / plo r9 / add16 r9, 1` — silently **overflows** when `max_clust` is already `$FFFF`: `$FFFF + 1` wraps to `$0000` in the 16-bit `R9` register, so `chk_wc_remaining` was initialized to exactly `0` for this card, on every single call, explaining every observed symptom in one shot — the 100% failure rate (every walk immediately hits "too long" before doing any work), `hops=0` universally, zero cross-link reports (no cluster is ever visited to begin with, let alone revisited), and the near-total "lost cluster" list (nothing is ever marked "seen," so every genuinely allocated cluster shows up as lost; the small gaps in the list are ordinary free clusters, not successfully-marked ones). This bug was invisible during design/static review because `+1` reads as an obviously-safe, deliberately-conservative one-hop safety margin — the overflow only manifests at the single boundary value `max_clust=$FFFF`, which this project's own prior hardware testing (`spc=128` cards) never happened to produce.
    - **Fix**: dropped the `add16 r9, 1` entirely — `chk_wc_remaining` is now set directly to `max_clust`. This is still provably sufficient: valid clusters run `2..max_clust` inclusive (`max_clust-1` distinct values), so a genuine non-cyclic chain can never need more than `max_clust-1` hops — using `max_clust` itself (not `max_clust+1`) keeps one full hop of slack while making the bound structurally unable to overflow, since `max_clust` is already representable in the same 16-bit width by construction. Updated `chk_walk_chain`'s own header comment and inline comments to document the bug and the fix in place, matching this project's own standing practice of leaving a bug-fix trail for exactly this class of mistake. All three temporary diagnostic blocks removed (`grep -n "TEMPORARY\|DIAGNOSTIC" progs/chkdsk.asm` confirmed clean).
    - **Verified**: clean assemble (0 errors/warnings), D-clobber sweep re-run clean (0 hits), no duplicate labels, full clean `make`+`make progs`+`make test` (kernel confirmed byte-unchanged — this is a pure `progs/chkdsk.asm` fix), `bin/chkdsk` relinked cleanly (`Lowest address: 4300` matching `PROG_BASE`, `'EDF'` magic confirmed).
    - **Second hardware round (2026-08-02, `chk3.txt`), across all 4 real partitions on the card (`C:`/`D:`/`E:`/`F:`, `sudo fsck.vfat -a` on each of the 4 host-side partitions as an independent cross-check)**: the overflow fix confirmed working (no more universal "too long" failures) — but a small, residual number of "Lost cluster" reports remained on **every** partition (`C:`=5, `D:`=2, `E:`=7, `F:`=7), and the user separately noticed `fsck.fat`'s own "N files" count is consistently 1 higher than `CHKDSK`'s own file+directory total (`C:` 158 vs 159, `D:` 1 vs 2, `E:` 24 vs 25, `F:` 43 vs 44) and asked whether the two might be related.
    - **The file-count "+1" is very likely a reporting-convention difference, not a scan bug, and probably unrelated to the lost-cluster residue**: the discrepancy is *exactly* `+1` on all four partitions regardless of real entry count (1 entry on `D:` up to 158 on `C:`) or subdirectory count (`F:` has 10 subdirectories yet still only `+1`, not `+11`) — a genuine "chkdsk skips one entry per directory level" bug would scale with the number of directory levels scanned, not stay flat. This flat, size-independent offset is the signature of `fsck.fat` counting the root directory itself as file #1 in its own summary line, a known dosfstools convention — not a missed `CHKDSK` scan.
    - **The lost-cluster numbers themselves are more telling**: they arrive in adjacent pairs plus occasional singles (`C:` `147,148`/`192,193` plus lone `144`; `E:` `19,20`/`72,73`/`82,83` plus lone `112`; `F:` `56,57`/`155,156` plus lone `135`/`148`/`167`) rather than scattered/random values — consistent with specific whole directory entries' cluster chains going completely unmarked (a 1- or 2-cluster file/directory whose entry is either never visited by the tree walk, or visited but never successfully chain-walked), not a byte-alignment/scan-boundary bug in the second-pass FAT scan itself (which would be expected to produce more randomly-distributed false hits).
    - **New `; TEMPORARY DIAGNOSTIC` added** at `chk_walk_dir`'s `cwd_not_dotdot:` (the point every real, non-`.`/`..` entry reaches during pass A's `K_DIR_READ` loop, for every directory level): prints `[E <name> c=<DIRENT_CLUST> a=<DIRENT_ATTR>]` for every single entry visited. This directly answers whether a "lost" cluster number ever shows up as some visited entry's own `c=` value (proving the entry *was* seen, so the bug is downstream in `chk_walk_chain`/`chk_mark_cluster` failing to mark part of its chain) or never appears at all among visited entries (meaning the owning entry itself is being skipped by the tree walk, a different bug in the scan/recursion logic). Verified: clean assemble (0 errors/warnings), D-clobber sweep 0 hits, no duplicate labels, full clean `make`+`make progs`+`make test` (kernel byte-unchanged, `bin/chkdsk` links at `Lowest address: 4300`).
    - **⭐ ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED (2026-08-02, `chk4.txt`) — a second, independent bug, unrelated to the earlier `max_clust+1` overflow.** The diagnostic build's output for `D:` (the smallest, cleanest test case) was fully decisive: `[E cfg c=3 a=16]` — the tree walk visited exactly one real entry, a subdirectory `cfg` at cluster 3, correctly attributed (`a=16`=`ATTR_DIR`) — yet CHKDSK still reported `Lost cluster: 2` and `Lost cluster: 5`. Since `cfg` (the only real entry on the whole drive) was demonstrably visited and its own cluster correctly marked "seen," the earlier "is the tree walk skipping real entries" hypothesis was directly disproven — the bug had to be in the *second pass* (`chk_fat_scan_lost`) itself, which independently re-scans the whole FAT table sequentially.
    - **Root cause, confirmed algebraically before touching any code**: `chk_fscan_cluster` (the cluster number being reported/checked) starts at `2` (deliberately skipping the two reserved clusters), while `chk_fscan_entry_idx` (the byte-offset index into whichever FAT sector is currently buffered) always starts/resets at `0` — and since the two counters only ever increment together, by exactly 1 each, they stay offset by a constant `+2` for the *entire* scan, not just near a boundary. Modeling it as `entry_idx(t) = t mod 256`, `sector_idx(t) = t div 256`, `cluster(t) = t + 2`: the real cluster actually being decoded is `sector_idx(t)*256 + entry_idx(t) = t` (a standard integer-division identity), while the cluster number being *reported and checked against the "seen" bitmap* is `t + 2` — a permanent, whole-scan `+2` mislabeling, independently verified via a small Python model across `t=0` through `t≈65533` before editing any assembly. This exactly explains `D:`'s two reports: `"Lost cluster: 2"` was really decoding cluster **0**'s own reserved (always-nonzero) FAT entry, mislabeled as `2`; `"Lost cluster: 5"` was really decoding cluster **3**'s own entry (`cfg`, correctly allocated and correctly marked "seen" under its real number) but checking the bitmap under the *wrong* label `5`, which was of course never marked. The earlier consecutive-pair patterns on the bigger drives (`C:`/`E:`/`F:`) are consistent with the same constant `+2` mislabeling landing on boundary transitions between a dense used-cluster range and free space.
    - **Fix**: `chk_fscan_cluster` now starts at `0`, tracked in perfect lockstep with `entry_idx` (offset always `0`, eliminating the mismatch at its root rather than patching around it), with an explicit, dedicated check inside `cfsl_entry_loop` (reusing `R9`, already freshly holding `cluster` from the `max_clust` bound check just above it — confirmed `SUB16`'s register-register form only ever writes back into its *first* operand, so `R9` survives untouched) that skips decoding/reporting/tallying entirely for cluster values `0` and `1` (falling straight through to the shared `cfsl_entry_done` increment-and-continue tail), rather than trying to start two counters at a deliberately mismatched offset. Also removed the now-fully-diagnosed `[E ...]` entry-listing diagnostic from `chk_walk_dir` (it did its job — proved the tree walk itself is correct — and is no longer needed).
    - **Verified**: independently re-derived the fixed model algebraically (`cluster(t)=t`, matching `real_cluster_read(t)=t` exactly, offset `0` throughout) before writing the fix. Clean assemble (0 errors/warnings), D-clobber sweep re-run clean (0 hits), no duplicate labels, full clean `make`+`make progs`+`make test` (kernel confirmed byte-unchanged), `bin/chkdsk` relinked cleanly (`Lowest address: 4300`, correct `'EDF'` magic). Hand-traced `D:`'s specific case against the fixed code: cluster 0/1 skipped; cluster 2 (if genuinely free on this card) now correctly tallies as free, not lost; cluster 3 (`cfg`'s real number) correctly reads its own entry and correctly finds the "seen" bit set — predicting **zero** lost-cluster reports for `D:` once fixed.
  - **✅ CONFIRMED WORKING ON HARDWARE (2026-08-02) — all four drives (`C:`/`D:`/`E:`/`F:`) now report a genuinely clean pass, matching `fsck.vfat`'s own independent check on every one.** This closes out the clean-volume side of `CHKDSK`'s hardware verification (both the `max_clust+1` overflow bug and the `chk_fat_scan_lost` off-by-2 bug are confirmed fixed, not just predicted).
    - **The file-count "+1 vs +11"-style comparisons the user raised turned out to be a pure reporting-terminology difference, fully reconciled**: `fsck.fat`'s single "N files" line is `real files + real (non-root) subdirectories + 1 (the root directory itself)`, all lumped into one number — while `CHKDSK` deliberately reports files and directories as two separate lines. Checked the exact arithmetic across all four drives (`chkdsk_files + chkdsk_dirs + 1`): `C:` `133+25+1=159`, `D:` `0+1+1=2`, `E:` `22+2+1=25`, `F:` `33+10+1=44` — every one matches `fsck.fat`'s reported count exactly, with nothing left over. No code change needed; `CHKDSK`'s own file/directory counts were correct the whole time.

- **Kernel size-reduction pass (Fable 5 subagent, isolated worktree) — 7 commits, CONFIRMED WORKING ON HARDWARE 2026-08-02.** Branch `redir-consolidation-agent`, worktree `.claude/worktrees/redir-consolidation` (zero risk to `main` by construction while pending — a hardware failure here would just have meant the branch doesn't merge). Directed in stages by the coordinator (the main session), each stage scoped narrowly given `kernel/redir.asm`'s own history as this project's worst hardware-bug file (multiple real register-preservation bugs found on hardware during the original I/O-redirection work) and the total absence of hardware/emulator access to validate a kernel change directly. **Hardware round**: installed and ran from this branch's own build (`PROG_BASE` still `$4400`, isolating this pass as the only variable, per the user's own explicit test-ordering decision), several commands exercised plus the existing batch test scripts (exercising `batch_mod.asm`'s own reloaded module, one of the 7 commits) — no issues found. Ready to merge to `main`.
  - **Three `kernel/redir.asm` dispatcher commits** (`fe03594`/`23e360e`/`d519895`): `_redir_type`/`_redir_msg`/`_redir_read` each had two call sites (their normal-success path and a discard/EOF path) ending in a byte-for-byte identical `pop`×N/`rtn` tail — hoisted into one shared physical copy reached via `lbr` from both sites, since `POP`'s own macro expansion never touches `DF` and neither branch needed any other register to survive into the tail. `_redir_type`/`_redir_msg` were pure mechanical hoists; `_redir_read` additionally had its success and `rrd_eof` paths route through a new shared `rrd_popret` by first stashing their respective D-values (the read byte, or a literal 0) into `R7` — this dispatcher's own established free-scratch register, confirmed against its own header (only `RF`/`RC`/`RA` are documented caller-visible). `_redir_inmsg` was surveyed and needed no change (its two paths already funnel into one shared `kim_restore` tail via existing `lbr`s); `rty_console`/`rmsg_console`/`rrd_console`'s own separate, longer tails (which do real extra work before falling into the raw BIOS call) were deliberately left unmerged. 31 bytes saved after `-r` relaxation (`Highest address` `$423e`→`$421f`).
  - **`progs/ls.asm` `ADD16`/`SUB16`-by-1/2 → `INC`/`DEC` sweep** (`22d4303`): the fallback target the coordinator authorized (`edlin.asm` or `ls.asm`, whichever had a clear, safe opportunity) once `redir.asm`'s own remaining candidates were exhausted — `edlin.asm` turned out already fully converted from its own 2026-07-30 pass (0 hits); `ls.asm` had 14 live sites (12 `±1`, 2 `±2`), each individually checked to confirm the following instruction always reloads `D` fresh or is an unconditional branch (never an `lbdf`/`lbnf` reading `DF` from the `add16`/`sub16` itself) before converting — `INC`/`DEC` set no flags at all, so this is only safe where nothing downstream depends on the carry. 103 bytes saved (`Code Generated` 4964→4861) — pure userland, zero kernel-resident impact, but frees program-RAM headroom `LS` itself has room to grow into.
  - **Two `kernel/file.asm` `file_write` consolidations** (`d97eefa`/`c2ab810`): the sector-cache-load block's success continuation (after `call f_ideread`) and the old `fwrite_ioerr_cleanup` label (reached via `lbdf` on that same call's failure) carried an identical `pop rc/pop rb/pop ra` sequence — moved to run once, unconditionally, immediately after the call and before the `DF` check (safe since `POP` never touches `DF`), with the check itself changed to `lbdf fwrite_ioerr` directly and the now-fully-dead `fwrite_ioerr_cleanup` label removed. The `f_idewrite`/`fwrite_ioerr_cleanup2` block got the identical treatment. The other five `lbdf fwrite_ioerr` sites in this same proc (`fat_alloc`/`fat_flush`/`fat_get`/`fat_set` at various points) were checked individually and are **not** duplicated this way — each already pops its protected registers exactly once, unconditionally, before its own single `lbdf`, with no separate success-path pop to collapse against — confirmed by tracing the proc's actual control flow rather than mechanically repeating the `redir.asm` pattern everywhere a `pop`-sequence appears. Callers checked too: every external caller reaches `file_write` only through `K_FILE_WRITE` (no documented register-survival contract beyond `RC`/`DF`); the only internal callers (`kernel/redir.asm`'s three dispatchers) already push/pop-protect everything they need and reload fresh afterward, so no caller depends on any register surviving through one exit path but not another. 38 bytes saved (`$421f`→`$420e`→`$41f9` across the two commits).
